\displaystyle\langle(\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M})^{*}(\alpha^{R}_{i}\otimes_{H}\alpha^{L}_{j}),m_{R}^{p}\otimes_{H}m_{L}^{q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha^{R}_{i}\otimes_{H}\alpha^{L}_{j},\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}(m_{R}^{p}\otimes_{H}m_{L}^{q})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha^{R}_{i}\otimes_{H}\alpha^{L}_{j},m_{L}^{q}\otimes_{H}m_{R}^{p}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta_{i}^{p}\delta_{j}^{q}

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha^{L}_{j}\otimes_{H}\alpha^{R}_{i},m_{R}^{p}\otimes_{H}m_{L}^{q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{*},M^{*}}(\alpha^{R}_{i}\otimes_{H}\alpha^{L}_{j}),m_{R}^{p}\otimes_{H}m_{L}^{q}\rangle

implies

(\overline{\sigma}_{M,M}^{\mathcal{W}})^{*}=\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{*},M^{*}}

by
H
-bilinearity and
non-degeneracy of the pairing.
∎

We now assume that
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}\colon M\otimes_{H}M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}M
is diagonalisable and that has

1
among its eigenvalues.
This is for example the case for all
quantum groups of the
A
,
B
,
C
,
D
series, with
M
the
bicovariant bimodule of
1
-forms, cf.
[
19
, Sec. 3]
,

[
11
, Prop. 3.1]
.
We denote by
V_{\lambda}\subseteq M
the eigenspace of

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}
with eigenvalue
\lambda\in\Lambda
. The
set
\Lambda
of eigenvalues is finite since
M^{2}
is a free and finitely
generated right
H
-module and
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}

is in particular right
H
-linear. We have

M^{2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}V_{\lambda}~~~\text{ and }~~~\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}=\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\lambda P_{\lambda}~,

(3.7)

where
P_{\lambda}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}
,
P_{\lambda}^{2}=P_{\lambda}

is the projector to the eigenspace
V_{\lambda}
corresponding to
the eigenvalue
\lambda
.
The explicit expression of the projectors is

P_{\nu}=\prod_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\nu\}}\frac{\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}-\lambda\mathbb{1}}{\nu-\lambda}~,\qquad\nu\in\Lambda~,

(3.8)

as is easily seen evaluating them on vectors
\omega_{\rho}\in V_{\rho}

for the two cases
\rho=\nu
and
\rho\not=\nu
. They are bicovariant
bimodules morphisms because compositions of bicovariant bimodule
morphisms.
The eigenspaces
V_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}(M^{2})
are bicovariant
bimodules since images of bicovariant bimodule morphisms, therefore
M^{2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}V_{\lambda}
is a direct
sum of bicovariant bimodules.

We define the vector subspaces

V^{*}_{\lambda}:=\{X\in(M^{*})^{2}~|~\langle X,V_{\rho}\rangle=0\text{ for all }\rho\neq\lambda\}

and

V_{\lambda}^{\perp}:=\{X\in(M^{*})^{2}~|~\langle X,V_{\lambda}\rangle=0\}

for all eigenvalues
\lambda\in\Lambda
. The vector subspace

V^{*}_{\lambda}\subseteq(M^{*})^{2}
is a subbicovariant bimodule, indeed,
since

V_{\lambda}
is a bicovariant bimodule
and the pairing is a morphism of bicovariant bimodules,

V^{*}_{\lambda}
is closed under the
H
-actions and the
H
-coactions.
Similarly
V_{\lambda}^{\perp}\subseteq(M^{*})^{2}
is a subbicovariant bimodule.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}
be diagonalisable with eigenvalues

\Lambda
, eigenspaces
V_{\lambda}
and projectors
P_{\lambda}

as in (
3.7
).
Then the following statements hold:

i.)

(M^{*})^{2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}V^{*}_{\lambda}

as bicovariant bimodules.

ii.)

The transpose of the braiding,

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{*},M^{*}}\colon(M^{*})^{2}\to(M^{*})^{2}
,
has the projector decomposition

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{*},M^{*}}=\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\lambda P^{*}_{\lambda}

where the projectors
P^{*}_{\lambda}:(M^{*})^{2}\to(M^{*})^{2}
to the
eigenspaces
V^{*}_{\lambda}
are the transpose of the projectors

P_{\lambda}
, i.e.,
P^{*}_{\lambda}=(P_{\lambda})^{*}
.

iii.)

For any
\lambda\in\Lambda
there is an isomorphism

V^{*}_{\lambda}\cong(V_{\lambda})^{*}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(V_{\lambda},H)

of bicovariant bimodules.

iv.)

For any
\lambda\in\Lambda
there is an isomorphism

V^{*}_{\lambda}\cong(M^{*})^{2}/V_{\lambda}^{\perp}
of bicovariant bimodules.

Proof.

i.)

V^{*}_{\lambda}\cap V^{*}_{\rho}=\{0\}
if
\lambda\neq\rho
,
by non-degeneracy of the evaluation. Therefore

\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}V^{*}_{\lambda}\subseteq(M^{*})^{2}
. To
show the other inclusion let
X\in(M^{*})^{2}
and

\omega=\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\omega_{\lambda}
be arbitrary,
where
\omega_{\lambda}\in V_{\lambda}
. Define
X_{\lambda}\in V^{*}_{\lambda}
by

X_{\lambda}(\omega_{\lambda})=X(\omega_{\lambda})
and
X_{\lambda}(\omega_{\rho})=0

for
\rho\neq\lambda
. This shows that
X
decomposes into a direct sum

X=\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
.

ii.)

The transpose
(P_{\nu})^{*}:(M^{*})^{2}\to(M^{*})^{2}
of the projector
P_{\nu}
satisfies, for any

X_{\nu}\in V^{*}_{\nu}
and
\omega_{\rho}\in V_{\rho}
with
\rho\not=\nu
,

\langle(P_{\nu})^{*}X_{\nu},\omega_{\rho}\rangle=\langle X_{\nu},P_{\nu}\omega_{\rho}\rangle=0
. Moreover,
for any

X_{\nu}\in V^{*}_{\nu}
and
\omega_{\nu}\in V_{\nu}
,

\langle(P_{\nu})^{*}X_{\nu},v_{\nu}\rangle=\langle X_{\nu},P_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}\rangle=\langle X_{\nu},\omega_{\nu}\rangle
shows that
(P_{\nu})^{*}

is the identity on
V_{\nu}^{*}
. Hence
(P_{\nu})^{*}
equals the
projector
P_{\nu}^{*}\colon(M^{*})^{2}\to(M^{*})^{2}
to the subspace
V_{\nu}^{*}
.
From Lemma
3.2
we have

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{*},M^{*}}=(\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M})^{*}=\big(\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\lambda P_{\lambda}\big)^{*}=\sum\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\lambda P^{*}_{\lambda}

as claimed.

iii.)

Every element of
(M^{*})^{2}\cong(M^{2})^{*}

can be restricted to an
H
-linear map
V_{\lambda}\rightarrow H
, so in particular
V^{*}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow(V_{\lambda})^{*}
,
\omega_{\lambda}\mapsto\langle\omega_{\lambda},\cdot\rangle
is an injection of bicovariant
bimodules. On the other hand, every

X\in(V_{\lambda})^{*}
can be extended to
(M^{2})^{*}\cong(M^{*})^{2}
by setting it
equal zero on all
V_{\rho}
for
\rho\neq\lambda
. By definition this extension is
in
V^{*}_{\lambda}\subseteq(M^{*})^{2}
and inverse to the
previous restriction, proving that the bicovariant bimodule injection

V^{*}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow(V_{\lambda})^{*}
,
\omega_{\lambda}\mapsto\langle\omega_{\lambda},\cdot\rangle
is an isomorphism.

iv.)

The quotient
(M^{2})^{*}/V_{\lambda}^{\perp}
is a
bicovariant bimodule, since it is a quotient of bicovariant bimodules. We prove that there is a non-degenerate pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}\colon(M^{*})^{2}/V_{\lambda}^{\perp}\otimes_{H}V_{\lambda}\rightarrow H~,~~~~~[X]\otimes_{H}\omega\mapsto\langle[X],\omega\rangle_{\lambda}:=\langle X,\omega\rangle

(3.9)

of bicovariant bimodules.
First of all, the
H
-bimodule map in (
3.9
) is well-defined since, if

[X]=0
any representative
X
is in
V_{\lambda}^{\perp}
and so

\langle X,V_{\lambda}\rangle=0
. The pairing is a morphism of bicovariant
bimodules since it is induced from the bicovariant bimodules pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
via a quotient of bicovariant
bimodules. Nondegeneracy follows from that of

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
.
This nondegenerate pairing implies that

(M^{2})^{*}/V_{\lambda}^{\perp}\cong(V_{\lambda})^{*}
and thus the quotient
is isomorphic to
V_{\lambda}^{*}
by
iii.)
.

∎

Recalling that the braided anti-symmetrization on
M^{2}
is

A_{2}={\rm id}_{M^{2}}-\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}
we
see that the eigenspace with eigenvalue
\lambda=1
is
V_{1}=S^{2}={\rm ker}A_{2}
, then
{\rm Im}A_{2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{1\}}V_{\lambda}
. It
is convenient to introduce the notation

M^{\vee 2}:=M\vee M:={\rm ker}A_{2}=S^{2}=V_{1}~.

We call
M^{\vee 2}
the
braided symmetric tensors
of

M^{2}
, and
M^{\wedge 2}=M\wedge M={\rm Im}A_{2}
the
braided antisymmetric tensors
, cf. (
3.4
).
From (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) we have in particular the direct sum of bicovariant bimodules

M^{2}=M^{\vee 2}\oplus M^{\wedge 2}

(3.10)

with the bicovariant bimodule morphisms

P_{\vee}:=P_{1}=\prod_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{1\}}\frac{\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}-\lambda\mathbb{1}}{1-\lambda}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad P_{\wedge}=\mathrm{id}_{M^{2}}-P_{\vee}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}~,

that are the
projectors to
M^{\vee 2}
and
M^{\wedge 2}
, respectively.

Dually, recalling Lemma (
3.3
) ii.) we set

(M^{*})^{\vee 2}:=M^{*}\vee M^{*}:={\rm ker}A_{2}^{*}=V_{1}^{*}~,~~(M^{*})^{\wedge 2}:=M^{*}\wedge M^{*}:={\rm Im}A^{*}_{2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{1\}}V^{*}_{\lambda}

and consider the direct sum decomposition

(M^{*})^{2}=(M^{*})^{\vee 2}\oplus(M^{*})^{\wedge 2}
.
Choosing
\lambda=1
in (
3.9
) we obtain the
nondegenerate pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{1}\colon(M^{*})^{2}/V_{1}^{\perp}\otimes_{H}M^{\vee 2}\rightarrow H
that composed with the isomorphism
V_{1}^{*}\cong(M^{*})^{2}/V_{1}^{\perp}
, cf. Lemma (
3.3
) iv.), gives
the nondegenerate pairing of braided symmetric tensors

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\vee}\colon(M^{*})^{\vee 2}\otimes_{H}M^{\vee 2}\rightarrow H~.

(3.11)

3.2
Bicovariant Differential Calculi and Structure Constants

Recall that a differential graded algebra
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})

is a (
\mathbb{N}_{0}
-)graded algebra

\Omega^{\bullet}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Omega^{k}

with graded product

\wedge\colon\Omega^{k}\otimes\Omega^{\ell}\rightarrow\Omega^{k+\ell}
,

k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a graded map

\mathrm{d}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet+1}
of degree
+1

(the
differential
), which
squares to zero (
\mathrm{d}^{2}=0
) and satisfies the

graded Leibniz rule

\mathrm{d}(\omega\wedge\eta)=\mathrm{d}\omega\wedge\eta+(-1)^{|\omega|}\omega\wedge\mathrm{d}\eta

for all homogeneous elements
\omega,\eta\in\Omega^{\bullet}
,
where
|\omega|\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
denotes the degree of
\omega

1
1

1

The graded algebra
\Omega^{\bullet}
is not necessarily
the braided exterior algebra

\Lambda^{\bullet}M
of a bicovariant bimodule
M
defined in
(
3.4
). Furthermore, the notation
\Omega^{\bullet}
and
\Omega^{k}
should not
be confused with the tensor product algebra
M^{\bullet}
,
M^{k}
.
.
A
differential calculus
on an algebra
A
is a differential graded
algebra
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
such that
\Omega^{0}=A
and

\Omega^{k}=\mathrm{span}_{\Bbbk}\{a^{0}\mathrm{d}a^{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}a^{k}~|~a^{0},\ldots,a^{k}\in A\}
for all
k>0
.
Note that the graded product is by assumption associative
but not graded commutative in general.
The data
(\Gamma:=\Omega^{1},\mathrm{d}\colon A\rightarrow\Gamma)
constitutes
a so-called
first order differential calculus
(for short FODC) on
A
, i.e.
an
A
-bimodule
\Gamma
together with a linear map

\mathrm{d}\colon A\rightarrow\Gamma
such that the
Leibniz rule

\mathrm{d}(ab)=(\mathrm{d}a)b+a\mathrm{d}b
holds for all
a,b\in A
and
the
surjectivity condition

A=\mathrm{span}_{\Bbbk}\{a\mathrm{d}b~|~a,b\in A\}
is satisfied.
The prototypical example of a differential calculus is the differential
graded algebra of differential forms
(\Omega^{\bullet}(M),\wedge,\mathrm{d})

on a smooth manifold
M
, with
\wedge
the wedge product and
\mathrm{d}
the
de Rham differential. The corresponding FODC is given by the smooth sections

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\infty}(T^{*}M)
of the cotangent bundle and the differential in
zero degree.
For a Lie group
G
we can use the pullback of the left and right multiplication on
G

to induce additional
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(G)
-coactions on
\Omega^{1}(G)
.
This example has the following noncommutative generalization. As before, we denote
by
H
a Hopf algebra (with invertible antipode).

Definition 3.4
.

A differential calculus
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
on
H

is said to be bicovariant if for all
k>0

and
a^{i,0},\ldots,a^{i,k}\in H
with

\sum_{i}a^{i,0}\mathrm{d}a^{i,1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}a^{i,k}=0

(finite sum over
i
) we have

\sum\nolimits_{i}\Delta(a^{i,0})(\mathrm{d}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta(a^{i,1}))\ldots(\mathrm{d}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta(a^{i,k}))=0=\sum\nolimits_{i}\Delta(a^{i,0})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{d})(\Delta(a^{i,1}))\ldots(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{d})(\Delta(a^{i,k}))~.

It is said to be right covariant if it satisfies the first equation and
left covariant if it satisfies the second equation.
A FODC is said to be bicovariant (right, left covariant) if the above conditions are satisfied in first degree.

The rationale of Definition
3.4
is the following.
To any bicovariant differential calculus
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
on
H

we associate two linear maps

\delta_{\Omega}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}\otimes H
and

\lambda_{\Omega}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow H\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}

by

\begin{split}\delta_{\Omega}(\omega)&=\sum\nolimits_{i}\Delta(a^{i,0})(\mathrm{d}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta(a^{i,1}))\ldots(\mathrm{d}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta(a^{i,k}))~,\\
\lambda_{\Omega}(\omega)&=\sum\nolimits_{i}\Delta(a^{i,0})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{d})(\Delta(a^{i,1}))\ldots(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{d})(\Delta(a^{i,k}))\end{split}

(3.12)

for all
k>0
and

\omega=\sum_{i}a^{i,0}\mathrm{d}a^{i,1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}a^{i,k}\in\Omega^{k}
, where
a^{i,0},\ldots,a^{i,k}\in H
.
Note that
\delta_{\Omega}
and
\lambda_{\Omega}
are well-defined through the
right and left covariance property of Definition
3.4
, respectively.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
be a differential calculus on
H
.
It is bicovariant if and only if
\Omega^{\bullet}
is a graded
H
-bicomodule algebra
and
\mathrm{d}
is
H
-bicolinear. In this case
the graded
H
-coactions are determined by (
3.12
).

See e.g.
[
21
, Sect. 12.2.3]
for a proof of
Proposition
3.5
.
As a corollary it follows that a FODC

(\Gamma,\mathrm{d})
on
H
is bicovariant if and only if
\Gamma
is a bicovariant
bimodule and
\mathrm{d}\colon H\rightarrow\Gamma
is an
H
-bicolinear map.
Given a bicovariant FODC
(\Gamma,\mathrm{d})
on
H
we can consider
the braided exterior algebra
(\Lambda^{\bullet}\Gamma,\wedge,1_{H})
of
Proposition
3.1
.

Proposition 3.6

(
[
33
]
Thm. 4.1)
.

Every bicovariant FODC
(\Gamma,\mathrm{d})
on
H
has a unique extension to a
bicovariant differential calculus
(\Lambda^{\bullet}\Gamma,\wedge,\mathrm{d})
.

A bicovariant differential calculus
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
will be called
Woronowicz differential calculus
if it equals the extension of of the bicovariant FODC
(\Gamma,\mathrm{d})
as in the above proposition. This will be the main examples of differential calculi in this paper.

We next recall the Cartan-Maurer formula associated with
(\Lambda^{\bullet}\Gamma,\wedge,\mathrm{d})

[
5
, Sect. 4]
(we use the conventions of vector fields acting from the left, cf.
[
6
]
). It will be used to define torsion-free connections in the next section.
By the Fundamental Theorem of Hopf Modules
(see Theorem
2.5
) it follows that the
H
-bimodule
\Gamma
of a bicovariant
FODC
(\Gamma,\mathrm{d})
on
H
is
H
-generated by
its coinvariant elements. Assume that
{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Gamma
is finite-dimensional and choose a basis
\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Gamma
.
There are linear maps
f_{j}{}^{i}\colon H\rightarrow\Bbbk

and elements
M_{j}{}^{i}\in H
such that

a\omega^{i}=\omega^{j}(f_{j}{}^{i}*a)~~~\text{ for all }a\in H~~~\text{ and }~~~\delta_{\Gamma}(\omega^{i})=\omega^{j}\otimes M_{j}{}^{i}~,

(3.13)

where we used Einstein sum convention and the convolution action

f_{j}{}^{i}*a:=a_{1}f_{j}{}^{i}(a_{2})
. It is easy to verify that

\omega^{i}a=(S(f_{j}{}^{i})*a)\omega^{j}
, where

S(f_{j}{}^{i})*a=a_{1}f_{j}{}^{i}(S(a_{2}))
,
and

\begin{split}\Delta(f_{j}{}^{i})&=f_{j}{}^{k}\otimes f_{k}{}^{i}~,~~~~\epsilon(f_{j}{}^{i})=\delta_{j}{}^{i}~,\\
\Delta(M_{j}{}^{i})&=M_{j}{}^{k}\otimes M_{k}{}^{i}~,~\epsilon(M_{j}{}^{i})=\delta_{j}{}^{i}\end{split}

for all
i,j\in I
. Moreover, we have the compatibility condition

(a*f_{i}{}^{j})M_{j}{}^{k}=M_{i}{}^{j}(f_{j}{}^{k}*a)

for all
a\in H
. We obtain a basis
\{\eta^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\Gamma^{\mathrm{co}H}

by defining
\eta^{i}=\omega^{j}S(M_{j}{}^{i})
. The braiding isomorphism

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma,\Gamma}\colon\Gamma\otimes_{H}\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma\otimes_{H}\Gamma
transforms basis elements as follows

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma,\Gamma}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\eta^{j})=\eta^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{i}
, since it is
H
-colinear it closes in

{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Gamma\otimes{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Gamma
,

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma,\Gamma}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega^{j})=\omega^{k}\otimes_{H}\omega^{\ell}\Sigma_{k\ell}{}^{ij}
.
From

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma,\Gamma}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega^{j})=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma,\Gamma}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\eta^{r}M_{r}{}^{j})=\eta^{r}\otimes_{H}(S(f_{k}{}^{i})*M_{r}{}^{j})\omega^{k}=\omega^{\ell}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}f_{k}{}^{i}S(M_{\ell}{}^{j})

we have
\Sigma_{k\ell}{}^{ij}=f_{\ell}{}^{i}(S(M_{k}{}^{j}))\in\Bbbk
.
Moreover,

\omega^{i}\wedge\omega^{j}=\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega^{j}-\omega^{k}\otimes_{H}\omega^{\ell}\Lambda_{k\ell}{}^{ij}~,

(3.14)

where
\Lambda=\Sigma^{-1}
,
hence
\Lambda_{k\ell}{}^{ij}:=f_{k}{}^{j}(M_{\ell}{}^{i})\in\Bbbk
.
Recall from Proposition
2.11
that
\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Gamma,H)
is
a bicovariant bimodule since we assume
\Gamma
to be finitely generated
as a right
H
-module. We call it the module of
vector fields

\mathfrak{X}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Gamma,H)

and consider the
basis
\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\mathfrak{X}

of left invariant elements dual to
\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
, i.e. such that

\langle\chi_{i},\omega^{j}\rangle=\delta_{i}^{j}
. We obtain

\chi_{i}a=(f_{i}{}^{j}*a)\chi_{j},~~~~~a\chi_{i}=\chi_{j}(\overline{S}(f_{i}{}^{j})*a),~~~~~\delta_{\mathfrak{X}}(\chi_{i})=\chi_{j}\otimes S(M_{i}{}^{j}),~~~~~\lambda_{\mathfrak{X}}(\chi_{i})=1\otimes\chi_{i}

for all
a\in H
and
i\in I
. In terms of the left invariant vector fields
and one forms we have

\mathrm{d}a=\omega^{i}(\chi_{i}*a)
.
We define the
structure constants

C^{i}_{kj}:=(\chi_{k}*\chi_{j})(x^{i})\in\Bbbk~,

where

x^{k}\in\ker(\epsilon)
such that
\chi_{i}(x^{k})=\delta_{i}^{k}
. From

0=\mathrm{d}^{2}a=\mathrm{d}(\omega^{i}(\chi_{i}*a))=\mathrm{d}(\omega^{i})(\chi*a)-\omega^{i}\wedge\omega^{j}(\chi_{j}*(\chi_{i}*a))~,

setting
a=x^{i}
in
\mathrm{d}^{2}(a_{2})\overline{S}(a_{1})

we obtain the
Cartan-Maurer formula

\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}=C^{i}_{kj}\omega^{j}\wedge\omega^{k}~.

(3.15)

Applying (
3.14
) we further deduce

\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}=C^{i}_{kj}(\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}-\Lambda_{mn}{}^{jk}\omega^{m}\otimes_{H}\omega^{n})=\mathbf{C}^{i}_{kj}\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}~,

(3.16)

where we defined the braided antisymmetric structure constants
\mathbf{C}^{i}_{kj}:=C^{i}_{kj}-\Lambda_{jk}{}^{mn}C^{i}_{nm}\in\Bbbk
.

4
Connections on Bicovariant Bimodules

We give a general introduction to connections on Hopf algebra modules
for covariant first order differential calculi (Section
4.1
),
their curvature and torsion (Section
4.2
) and duality of connections
(Section
4.3
). We illustrate all notions via concrete canonical examples
on bicovariant bimodules. In particular, we prove that there are
flat connections on any bicovariant bimodule and torsion-free connections on those of one forms and vector fields. Utilizing the tensor product
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
of rational morphisms,
we define the sum of connections in Section
4.4
, their curvatures are studied in Section
4.5
.

4.1
Definitions and Canonical Examples

Fix
a bicovariant differential calculus
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})

on
H
.

Definition 4.1
.

A right connection on a right
H
-module
M
is a linear map

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
satisfying

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(ma)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

for all
m\in M
and
a\in H
. The set of all right connections on
M
is denoted by

\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
.

A left connection on a left
H
-module
M
is a linear map

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M
satisfying

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(am)=a{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(m)+\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m

for all
m\in M
and
a\in H
. The set of all left connections on
M
is denoted by

{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
.

Given a right
H
-module
M
there is a free and transitive right action

\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)\times\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)~,~~~~~({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!},\phi)\mapsto{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}+\phi~.

of
\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
on
\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

turning
\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
into
an affine space over
\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
.
Similarly, for
M
a left
H
-module,
{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
becomes an affine space over
{}_{H}\mathrm{Hom}(M,\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M)
.
On covariant modules there always exist connections. We give their explicit form in the
following lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

cc

i.)

For any module
M
of
\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
there is a
right connection

\mathrm{d}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

defined by

\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{2}

(4.1)

for all
m\in M
.

ii.)

For any module
M
of
{}_{H}\mathcal{M}^{H}
there is a
left connection

{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)

defined by
{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}(m)=\mathrm{d}m_{2}\otimes_{H}\overline{S}(m_{1})m_{0}

for all
m\in M
.

iii.)

For any module
M
of
{}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}
there is a
right connection

\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

defined by
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}(m)=m_{0}\overline{S}(m_{-1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{-2}

for all
m\in M
.

iv.)

For any module
M
of
{}_{H}^{H}\mathcal{M}
there is a
left connection

{}^{M}\mathrm{d}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)

defined by
{}^{M}\mathrm{d}(m)=\mathrm{d}m_{-2}\otimes_{H}S(m_{-1})m_{0}

for all
m\in M
.

Proof.

We prove that (
4.1
) defines a right connection on
M
. In fact

\mathrm{d}^{M}(ma)=m_{0}a_{1}S(m_{1}a_{2})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(m_{2}a_{3})=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes\mathrm{d}(m_{2})a+m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}\mathrm{d}a=\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

for all
m\in M
and
a\in H

by the Leibniz rule of
\mathrm{d}
and the right covariance of
M
.
The statements about

{}^{M}\mathrm{d}
,
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
and

{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}
follow in complete analogy.
∎

The connection
\mathrm{d}^{M}
is the unique right connection that vanishes
on
M^{\mathrm{co}H}
. Similarly,
{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}
is the unique
left connection that vanishes on
M^{\mathrm{co}H}
. Using
{}^{\mathrm{co}H}M

instead of
M^{\mathrm{co}H}
leads to the connections
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}

and
{}^{M}\mathrm{d}
.
We sometimes refer to the connections of Lemma
4.2
as the

canonical connections
. These are easily proven to be
H
-bicolinear maps
and so in particular rational morphisms according to Lemma
2.2
.
Together with the fact that connections form an affine space over right

H
-linear maps (which we proved in Proposition
2.11
to be rational)
this implies that any connection on a finitely generated
covariant module is rational.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
M
be a finitely generated right
H
-module.

i.)

If
M
is a module of
\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
then there is the set inclusion

\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)\subset\mathrm{HOM}^{\mathrm{Ad}}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
,
i.e. every right connection on
M
is a right rational morphism.

ii.)

If
M
is a module of
{}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}
then there is the set inclusion

\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)\subset{}^{\mathrm{Ad}}\mathrm{HOM}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
,
i.e. every right connection on
M
is a left rational morphism.

iii.)

If
M
is a module of
{}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
then there is the set inclusion

\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)\subset\mathrm{HOM}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
,
i.e. every right connection on
M
is a rational morphism.

Proof.

Consider a right covariant right module
M
and the right connection

\mathrm{d}^{M}
on
M
presented in Lemma
4.2
i.). Recalling the
definition of
\delta^{\mathrm{Ad}}
in (
2.1
) we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathrm{Ad}}(\mathrm{d}^{M})(m)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}^{M}(m_{0})_{0}\otimes\mathrm{d}^{M}(m_{0})_{1}S(m_{1})

\displaystyle=(m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{2})_{0}\otimes(m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{2})_{1}S(m_{3})

\displaystyle=m_{0}S(m_{2})_{1}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{3}\otimes m_{1}S(m_{2})_{2}m_{4}S(m_{5})

\displaystyle=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\otimes 1_{H}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\otimes 1_{H}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}^{M}\otimes 1_{H})(m)

for all
m\in M
, that is,

\delta^{\mathrm{Ad}}(\mathrm{d}^{M})=\mathrm{d}^{M}\otimes 1_{H}
. Hence

\mathrm{d}^{M}\in\mathrm{HOM}^{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
, in particular it
is right rational.
Since any right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
on
M
equals
\mathrm{d}^{M}+\phi
for
a unique right
H
-linear map
\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

the result
i.)
follows from Proposition
2.11

i.)
, which states
that right rationality of
\phi
holds if

M
is a finitely generated right
H
-module.
Similarly, the right connection
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
of
Lemma
4.2
iii.) satisfies
\lambda^{\mathrm{Ad}}(\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}})=1_{H}\otimes\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
and from Proposition
2.11

ii.)

we conclude that any right connection is left rational, i.e.
ii.)
.
The last statement of the proposition follows from
i.)
and
ii.)
.
∎

There is an analogue version of Proposition
4.3
for
the left connections of Lemma
4.2
ii.) and iv.).
In this case rationality is with respect to the internal
\mathrm{Hom}
-functors

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{HOM}}^{\mathrm{Ad}}
,

{}^{\mathrm{Ad}}\overleftarrow{\mathrm{HOM}}
and

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{HOM}}
of morphisms ’acting from the right’ defined after
Proposition
2.3
.

Remark 4.4
.

The coactions
\delta^{\mathrm{Ad}}
and
\lambda^{\mathrm{Ad}}
do
not restrict to coactions on
\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
,
which is an affine space, not a vector space.
Let
M
be a finitely generated right
H
-module.
If
M
is a module of
\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
then

\delta^{\mathrm{Ad}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{1}\in\mathrm{HOM}^{\mathrm{Ad}}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})\otimes H
satisfies the
pseudo Leibniz rule

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(ma)\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{1}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(m)a\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{1}+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a\otimes 1_{H}

for all
m\in M
and
a\in H
. This is the case since

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(ma)\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{1}

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0}a_{1})_{0}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0}a_{1})_{1}S(m_{1}a_{2})

\displaystyle=({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0})a_{1})_{0}\otimes({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0})a_{1})_{1}S(m_{1}a_{2})+(m_{0}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a_{1})_{0}\otimes(m_{0}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a_{1})_{1}S(m_{1}a_{2})

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0})_{0}a_{1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{0})_{1}a_{2}S(m_{1}a_{3})+m_{0}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a_{1}\otimes m_{1}a_{2}S(m_{1}a_{3})

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(m)a\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{1}+m_{0}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a\otimes 1_{H}

by the Leibniz rule of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
. Similarly one proves that if

M
is a module of
{}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}
finitely generated as a right
H
-module
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
then

\lambda^{\mathrm{Ad}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{-1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}

satisfies the pseudo Leibniz rule

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{-1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(ma)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{-1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{0}(m)a+1_{H}\otimes m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

(4.2)

for all
m\in M
and
a\in H
.

We fix a bicovariant bimodule
M
for the rest of this section.
We observe that the right connection

\mathrm{d}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
of Lemma
4.2

i.)
satisfies
the additional Leibniz rule

\mathrm{d}^{M}(am)=a_{1}m_{0}S(a_{2}m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(a_{3}m_{2})=a\cdot\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},M}(\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m)

for all
a\in H
and
m\in M
, where
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
denotes the braiding of
bicovariant bimodules defined in (
2.9
). It is therefore a

bimodule connection
(cf.
[
17
,
8
]
).

Definition 4.5
.

A right connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
is said to be a bimodule right connection
if

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(am)=a{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},M}(\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m)

(4.3)

for all
a\in H
and
m\in M
, while a left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
is said to be a bimodule left connection
if

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(ma)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)a+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,\Omega^{1}}(m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a)

(4.4)

for all
a\in H
and
m\in M
. There are obvious versions of

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
-bimodule connections if we replace

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}

by
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
in (
4.3
) and (
4.4
).

For bimodule connections on bicovariant bimodules there is an immediate
correspondence between right and left connections.

Remark 4.6
.

If
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
is a bimodule right connection, then

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}}:=\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,\Omega^{1}}\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M

is a
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
-bimodule left connection. Namely,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}}(am)=a{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}}(m)+\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m\quad\text{ and }\quad{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}}(ma)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}}(m)a+\overline{\sigma}_{M,\Omega^{1}}^{\mathcal{W}}(m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a)

for all
a\in H
and
m\in M
. Conversely, a

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
-bimodule left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M
gives rise to a bimodule right connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\sigma}:=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},M}\circ{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
. Those constructions are inverse to each other,
i.e.
({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{\sigma}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
and

({\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\sigma})^{\overline{\sigma}}={\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}
, as one easily verifies.
There is an analogous correspondence between
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
-bimodule
right connections and bimodule left connections.

Example 4.7
.

The canonical connections
\mathrm{d}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and

{}^{M}\mathrm{d}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
are bimodule
right and left connections, respectively. The corresponding

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
-bimodule left and right connections are

{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}=(\mathrm{d}^{M})^{\overline{\sigma}}\colon M\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}=({}^{M}\mathrm{d})^{\sigma}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}.

If
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
is a Woronowicz differential
calculus on a braided exterior algebra of Proposition
3.6

we can use the structure constants to construct a right connection

\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
.
Recall that, given a finite-dimensional basis
\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of

{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Omega^{1}
, the structure constants
C^{i}_{kj}
,

\mathbf{C}^{i}_{kj}\in\Bbbk
are determined via the Cartan-Maurer formulas
(
3.15
) and (
3.16
).
We define two linear maps

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c},\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}

by

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i})=-\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}(\omega^{i})=-\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}\mathbf{C}^{i}_{kj}=-\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}

(4.5)

for all
i\in I
and extend those maps as right connections
on the free right
H
-module
\Omega^{1}={}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Omega^{1}\otimes H
.
Namely,
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}a_{i})=-\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}a_{i}+\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a_{i}

for all
a_{i}\in H
and similarly for
\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}
.
In the following we call
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
and
\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}
the

structure constants connections
.
Whenever discussing them we implicitly assume a Woronowicz differential calculus.

Lemma 4.8
.

The structure constants connections are left
H
-coinvariant elements, i.e.,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c},\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}\in{}^{H}\mathrm{Hom}(\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
.

Proof.

First note that the structure constant connections are left rational by
Proposition
4.3
.
We prove that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
is left
H
-coinvariant. Then it is clear that

\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}
is left
H
-coinvariant, as well.
For any
\omega\in\Omega^{1}
and a basis
\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of

{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Omega^{1}
there are unique
a_{i}\in H
such that

\omega=\omega^{i}a_{i}
by the Fundamental Theorem of Hopf Modules. Then

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}_{-1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}_{0}(\omega)

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega_{0})_{-1}\overline{S}(\omega_{-1})\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega_{0})_{0}

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}(a_{i})_{2})_{-1}\overline{S}((a_{i})_{1})\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}(a_{i})_{2})_{0}

\displaystyle=-(\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}(a_{i})_{2})_{-1}\overline{S}((a_{i})_{1})\otimes(\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}(a_{i})_{2})_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(a_{i})_{2})_{-1}\overline{S}((a_{i})_{1})\otimes(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(a_{i})_{2})_{0}

\displaystyle=-(a_{i})_{2}\overline{S}((a_{i})_{1})\otimes\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}(a_{i})_{3}+(a_{i})_{2}\overline{S}((a_{i})_{1})\otimes\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(a_{i})_{3}

\displaystyle=1_{H}\otimes(-\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}a_{i}+\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a_{i})

\displaystyle=1_{H}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega)

follows.
∎

If we extend the linear maps in (
4.5
) as left connections we similarly obtain two
left
H
-coinvariant left connections.

Considering structure constants with respect to a
basis
\{\eta^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of right
H
-coinvariant elements of
\Omega^{1}

leads to yet another pair of right/left connections, this time right
H
-coinvariant.
In the following we mainly focus on
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
and
\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}
.

4.2
Curvature and Torsion

Let
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
be a bicovariant differential calculus
over
H
. Using the differential
\mathrm{d}
we extend any right
connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
on a right
H
-module

M
to a linear map
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\colon M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet+1}
by

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}(m\otimes_{H}\omega)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)\wedge\omega+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}\omega

(4.6)

for
m\in M
and
\omega\in\Omega^{\bullet}
.
Note that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}

is well-defined on the balanced tensor product
M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}
since

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(ma)\wedge\omega+ma\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}\omega=({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a)\wedge\omega+m\otimes_{H}a\mathrm{d}\omega={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)\wedge a\omega+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(a\omega)

for
a\in H
by the Leibniz rules of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
and
\mathrm{d}
.
For a left connection
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)

on a left
H
-module
M
we define a linear map

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\otimes_{H}M\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet+1}\otimes_{H}M

by

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}(\omega\otimes_{H}m)=\omega\wedge{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(m)+(-1)^{|\omega|}\mathrm{d}\omega\otimes_{H}m

(4.7)

where

\omega\in\Omega^{k}
,
|\omega|=k
and

m\in M
. From

\displaystyle\omega a\wedge{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(m)+(-1)^{|\omega a|}\mathrm{d}(\omega a)\otimes_{H}m

\displaystyle=\omega\wedge a{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(m)+(-1)^{|\omega|}\mathrm{d}(\omega)a\otimes_{H}m+(-1)^{2|\omega|}\omega\wedge\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m

\displaystyle=\omega\wedge{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(am)+(-1)^{|\omega|}\mathrm{d}\omega\otimes_{H}am~,

where
a\in H
,
we see that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}
is well-defined on the balanced tensor product

\Omega^{\bullet}\otimes_{H}M
.

Lemma 4.9
.

The extension
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\colon M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet+1}
of a right connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
on a right
H
-module
M
satisfies the
graded Leibniz rule

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{\bullet}((m\otimes_{H}\omega)\cdot a)=({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{\bullet}(m\otimes_{H}\omega))\cdot a+(-1)^{|\omega|}m\otimes_{H}\omega\wedge\mathrm{d}a~.

The extension
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\otimes_{H}M\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet+1}\otimes_{H}M
of a left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
on a left
H
-module
M
satisfies the
graded Leibniz rule

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}^{\bullet}(a\cdot(\omega\otimes_{H}m))=a\cdot{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}^{\bullet}(\omega\otimes_{H}m)+(-1)^{|\omega|}\mathrm{d}a\wedge\omega\otimes_{H}m~,

for all
m\in M
,
\omega\in\Omega^{\bullet}
and
a\in H
.

Remark 4.10
.

Let
M
be a module of
{}_{H}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
, finitely generated as a
right
H
-module and consider a connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
.
Using the tensor product
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
introduced in Theorem
2.14
we have two well-defined linear maps

\wedge_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}),\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}\colon M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet+1}
, and their sum equals
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\colon M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet+1}
.
Indeed

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}=\wedge_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{\bullet}})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}

(4.8)

is an equation of linear maps

M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet}\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{\bullet+1}
,
where all individual terms are well-defined.

Now consider a left connection
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)

on
M
.
Since left connection are understood as maps “acting from the right”,
by employing the analogue of
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
in
\overleftarrow{\mathrm{HOM}}
we can define
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}
as a sum of well-defined linear maps
\Omega^{\bullet}\otimes_{H}M\to\Omega^{\bullet+1}\otimes_{H}M
.
Since we are mainly interested in the theory of right connections we are not going
to spell this out in detail.

The extensions of a connection are used to define the curvature
as the square of the connection.

Definition 4.11

(Curvature)
.

The curvature of a
right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
on a right
H
-module
M
is
by definition the linear map

\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{\bullet}\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2}~,

(4.9)

while the curvature of a
left connection
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(M)
on a left
H
-module
M
is
by definition the linear map

\mathrm{R}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}={\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}^{\bullet}\circ{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}M~.

A connection with zero curvature is said to be flat.

The following lemma shows that these curvatures are right and left
H
-linear maps, respectively. Hence, they determine assignments

\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2})

between the affine spaces of connections and the
vector spaces
\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2})
and
{}_{H}\mathrm{Hom}(M,\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}M)
.

Lemma 4.12
.

The curvature

\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2})
of a right connection
is right
H
-linear and the
curvature
\mathrm{R}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Hom}(M,\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}M)

of a left connection is left
H
-linear.

For connections on
\Omega^{1}
or the dual bicovariant bimodule
\mathfrak{X}

there is a natural definition of torsion. In the following

\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
denotes a basis of the vector space

{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Omega^{1}
of left coinvariant elements, that we call
left-invariant following
[
33
]
. Let

\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be the corresponding dual basis of
{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\mathfrak{X}
.
We further abbreviate
\mathrm{I}:=\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\chi_{i}\in\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}
, where the sum over repeated indices is understood.
Recall that
\wedge\colon\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{2}
,

\omega\otimes_{H}\eta\mapsto\omega\wedge\eta
is the product of the bicovariant differential calculus

(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
.

Definition 4.13

(Torsion)
.

The torsion of a right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})

on
\Omega^{1}
is the right
H
-linear map

\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}:=\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}+\mathrm{d}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{2}~.

Using the dual module
\mathfrak{X}
,
\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}\in\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}
.
The torsion of a left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(\mathfrak{X})
on

\mathfrak{X}
is the
\mathfrak{X}
-valued
2
-form

\mathrm{Tor}^{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}}:={\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}(\mathrm{I})\in\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}~.

A connection with zero torsion is said to be torsion-free.

In analogy to Lemma
4.12
we have that the torsion of a right connection
corresponds to an assignment
\mathrm{Tor}\colon\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1},\Omega^{2})
.

As an example we calculate curvature and torsion of the connections
introduced in the previous section. It turns out that the canonical connections
are all flat with non-trivial torsion that is easily computable, while
the structure
constants connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
is torsion-free and non-flat.

Proposition 4.14
.

i.)

The canonical connections defined in Proposition
4.2
are flat.

ii.)

The structure constants connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
of
Proposition
4.8
is torsion-free with curvature

\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}(\omega^{i})=(C^{i}_{kj}C^{j}_{nm}-C^{i}_{\ell m}C^{\ell}_{kn})\omega^{m}\otimes_{H}\omega^{n}\wedge\omega^{k}~.

Proof.

Since
M\cong M^{\mathrm{co}H}\otimes H
,
\mathrm{d}^{M}(M^{\mathrm{co}H})=0

and
\mathrm{R}^{\mathrm{d}^{M}}
is right
H
-linear we have

\mathrm{R}^{\mathrm{d}^{M}}=0
.
Similarly for the other canonical connections.

Next we consider the right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}

on
\Omega^{1}
. By the Cartan-Maurer formula (
3.15
)

\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}(\omega^{i})=-\omega^{j}\wedge\omega^{k}C^{i}_{kj}+\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}=0,

which implies
\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}=0
since
\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}
is
right
H
-linear.
The curvature of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}}(\omega^{i})

\displaystyle=({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c})^{\bullet}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}))

\displaystyle=-C^{i}_{kj}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c})^{\bullet}(\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k})

\displaystyle=-C^{i}_{kj}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{j})\wedge\omega^{k}-C^{i}_{kj}\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}\omega^{k}

\displaystyle=C^{i}_{kj}C^{j}_{nm}\omega^{m}\otimes_{H}\omega^{n}\wedge\omega^{k}-C^{i}_{kj}C^{k}_{sr}\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{r}\wedge\omega^{s}~.

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.3
Dual Connection

We recall the duality of left and right modules in a more general setting than that in
Section
3.1
on bicovariant bimodules. We then study dual connections.

Definition 4.15
.

A right
H
-module
M
and a left
H
-module
N

are said to be dual if there is an
H
-bilinear map

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon N\otimes M\rightarrow H

which is non-degenerate, i.e.
\langle n,m\rangle=0

for all
n\in N
implies
m=0
and

\langle n,m\rangle=0
for all
m\in M
implies
n=0
.

If
M
is a finitely generated projective right
H
-module,
the vector space
M^{*}=\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,H)
of right

H
-linear maps
M\rightarrow H
becomes a left
H
-module.
The left
H
-action on

M^{*}
is defined by
(a\cdot\alpha)(m)=a\cdot\alpha(m)

for all
a\in H
,
m\in M
and
\alpha\in M^{*}
.
The pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon M^{*}\otimes M\rightarrow H
is the
evaluation.

Proposition 4.16
.

Consider a dual pair
(M,N,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle)
,
as given in Definition
4.15
and a differential calculus

(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
on
H
.

i.)

Any right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
induces a left connection
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(N)
, called the dual connection, via the formula

\mathrm{d}\langle n,m\rangle=\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle+\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle

(4.10)

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
.

ii.)

Conversely, any left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(N)

induces a dual right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
via eq.(
4.10
).

iii.)

If
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(N)
are connections dual to each other, i.e.,
such that eq.(
4.10
) holds, the curvatures

\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2}
and

\mathrm{R}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon N\rightarrow\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}N

are dual to each other in the sense that

\langle n,\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}m\rangle=\langle\mathrm{R}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle

holds for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
.

iv.)

If
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})

is a right connection and
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(\mathfrak{X})
is the dual left connection,
the torsions

\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{2}
and

\mathrm{Tor}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in\Omega^{2}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}

are dual to each other in the sense that

-\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}(\omega)=\langle\mathrm{Tor}^{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}},\omega\rangle

(4.11)

holds for all
\omega\in\Omega^{1}
.

Proof.

Let
m\in M
,
n\in N
and
a\in H
.

i.)

Assume that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
is a right connection on
M
. Then

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\colon N\rightarrow\Omega\otimes_{H}N
is well-defined
via the pairing, since

\displaystyle\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\cdot a\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\langle n,m\cdot a\rangle-\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m\cdot a)\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}(\langle n,m\rangle a)-\langle n,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m)\cdot a\rangle-\langle n,m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a\rangle

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}\langle n,m\rangle)\cdot a+\langle n,m\rangle\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a-\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle\cdot a-\langle n,m\rangle\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle\cdot a~.

It is a left connection on
N
, since

\displaystyle\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(a\cdot n),m\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\langle a\cdot n,m\rangle-\langle a\cdot n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}(a\langle n,m\rangle)-a\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}\langle n,m\rangle+a\cdot\mathrm{d}\langle n,m\rangle-a\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}n,m\rangle+a\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}n,m\rangle+\langle a\cdot{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle~.

ii.)

This follows in complete analogy to
i.)
.

iii.)

If
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
and
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}
are right and left connections, respectively,
such that eq.(
4.10
) is satisfied, then

\displaystyle\langle n,\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}m\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{\bullet}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m))\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{\bullet}(m_{M}\otimes_{H}m_{\Omega})

\displaystyle=\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m_{M})\wedge m_{\Omega}\rangle+\langle n,m_{M}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{\Omega}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m_{M}\rangle\wedge m_{\Omega}+\langle n,m_{M}\rangle\cdot\mathrm{d}m_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\langle n,m_{M}\rangle\wedge m_{\Omega}-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m_{M}\rangle\wedge m_{\Omega}+\mathrm{d}(\langle n,m_{M}\rangle\cdot m_{\Omega})-\mathrm{d}(\langle n,m_{M}\rangle)\wedge m_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m_{M}\rangle\wedge m_{\Omega}+\mathrm{d}(\langle n,m_{M}\rangle\cdot m_{\Omega})

\displaystyle=-n_{\Omega}\wedge\langle n_{N},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle+\mathrm{d}\langle n,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m\rangle

\displaystyle=-n_{\Omega}\wedge\mathrm{d}\langle n_{N},m\rangle+n_{\Omega}\wedge\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n_{N},m\rangle+\mathrm{d}^{2}\langle n,m\rangle-\mathrm{d}\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n,m\rangle

\displaystyle=-n_{\Omega}\wedge\mathrm{d}\langle n_{N},m\rangle+n_{\Omega}\wedge\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n_{N},m\rangle-\mathrm{d}(n_{\Omega}\cdot\langle n_{N},m\rangle)

\displaystyle=n_{\Omega}\wedge\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n_{\mathcal{N}},m\rangle-\mathrm{d}(n_{\Omega})\cdot\langle n_{N},m\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle n_{\Omega}\wedge{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n_{N}+(-1)^{1}\mathrm{d}n_{\Omega}\otimes_{H}n_{N},m\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{R}^{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(n),m\rangle~,

where we used the short notation
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}m=m_{M}\otimes_{H}m_{\Omega}
and

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}n=n_{\Omega}\otimes_{H}n_{N}
.

iv.)

Let
\omega=\omega^{i}a_{i}\in\Omega^{1}
and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\omega=\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega^{j}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{ij}\in\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
,
where
a_{i},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{ij}\in H
are suitable coefficients with respect to the bases

\omega^{i}
and
\omega^{i}\otimes\omega^{j}
of left-invariant one forms. Then

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{Tor}^{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}},\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}(\omega^{i}\otimes\chi_{i}),\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\omega^{i}\wedge{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(\chi_{i})+(-1)^{|\omega^{i}|}\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\chi_{i},\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{i}\wedge\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(\chi_{i}),\omega\rangle-\mathrm{d}\omega^{i}\langle\chi_{i},\omega^{j}a_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega^{i}\wedge(\mathrm{d}\langle\chi_{i},\omega^{j}a_{j}\rangle-\langle\chi_{i},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\omega\rangle)-\mathrm{d}(\omega^{i})a_{i}

\displaystyle=\omega^{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}a_{i}-\omega^{i}\wedge\langle\chi_{i},\omega^{j}\otimes_{H}\omega^{k}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{jk}\rangle-\mathrm{d}(\omega^{i})a_{i}

\displaystyle=-\omega^{i}\wedge\omega^{k}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{ik}-\mathrm{d}(\omega^{i}a_{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Tor}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}(\omega)~.

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Note that the minus sign in (
4.11
) appears because torsion is of
odd degree, while curvature is of even degree.

We already noticed in Example
4.7
that
{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}

is the left connection corresponding to the right connection
\mathrm{d}^{M}

and
{}^{M}\mathrm{d}
is the left connection corresponding to the right
connection
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
via composition with the braiding

\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
. In the following proposition we prove that
these connections are furthermore dual to each other.
We also construct the connections dual to the structure constants connections of
Proposition
4.8
.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
M
be a bicovariant bimodule and

(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
a bicovariant
differential calculus on
H
.
Then

\mathrm{d}\langle\alpha,m\rangle=\langle{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}(\alpha),m\rangle+\langle\alpha,\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\rangle~~,~~~\mathrm{d}\langle\alpha,m\rangle=\langle{}^{M}\mathrm{d}(\alpha),m\rangle+\langle\alpha,\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}(m)\rangle

holds for all
m\in M
and
\alpha\in M^{*}
, i.e.

\mathrm{d}^{M}
and
{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}
are dual connections,
and
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
and
{}^{M}\mathrm{d}
are dual connections.

Furthermore, the left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{c}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(\mathfrak{X})
dual to

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
is given by

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{c}(a^{i}\chi_{i})=a^{i}C^{k}_{ni}\omega^{n}\otimes_{H}\chi_{k}+\mathrm{d}a^{i}\otimes_{H}\chi_{i}

(4.12)

and the left connection

\widetilde{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}}{}^{c}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(\mathfrak{X})
dual to

\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}{}^{c}
is given by

\widetilde{{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}}{}^{c}(a^{i}\chi_{i})=a^{i}\mathbf{C}^{k}_{ni}\omega^{n}\otimes_{H}\chi_{k}+\mathrm{d}a^{i}\otimes_{H}\chi_{i}

for all
a^{i}\in H
, with
\{\chi_{i}\}
the basis dual to
\{\omega^{i}\}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathrm{d}^{M}
and
{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}
have the defining property of vanishing on right invariant elements. Then
\mathrm{d}\langle\alpha,m\rangle=\langle{}^{\overline{M}}\mathrm{d}(\alpha),m\rangle+\langle\alpha,\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\rangle
trivially holds if
\alpha
and
m
are right invariant, since all terms vanish. The duality then follows recalling that
M\cong M^{\mathrm{co}H}\otimes H
and
M^{*}\cong H\otimes(M^{*})^{\mathrm{co}H}
.
Similarly, using
M\cong H\otimes{}^{\mathrm{co}H}M
and
M^{*}\cong{}^{\mathrm{co}H}(M^{*})\otimes H
,
one proves that
\mathrm{d}^{\overline{M}}
and
{}^{M}\mathrm{d}
are dual.
Moreover,

\mathrm{d}\langle\chi_{i},\omega^{j}\rangle-\langle\chi_{i},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{j})\rangle=\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\chi_{i}),\omega^{j}\rangle

similarly implies that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}^{c}
defined in (
4.12
) is the dual connection of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
.
The dual connection of
\widetilde{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}^{c}
is obtained by substituting

C^{i}_{jk}
with
\mathbf{C}^{i}_{jk}
.
∎

4.4
Sum of Connections

Given two right connections on modules
M
,
N
we construct their sum
which is a
a right connection on the tensor product module
M\otimes N
.
In the following
M
and
N
denote bicovariant bimodules.
We first study the relation between the canonical connections on
M
,
N
and

M\otimes_{H}N
.

Lemma 4.18
.

The canonical connections on
M
,
N
and
M\otimes_{H}N
are related via

\mathrm{d}^{M\otimes_{H}N}(m\otimes_{H}n)=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}^{N}(n),

(4.13)

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
.

Proof.

Let
m\in M
and
n\in N
. We have

\displaystyle\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}(\mathrm{d}m_{2}\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}n_{0}S(n_{1})\otimes_{H}S(m_{3})\mathrm{d}m_{4}n_{2}

\displaystyle=m_{0}\otimes_{H}n_{0}S(n_{1})\otimes_{H}S(m_{1})\mathrm{d}m_{2}n_{2}~,

which implies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{M\otimes_{H}N}(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=(m_{0}\otimes_{H}n_{0})S(m_{1}n_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(m_{2}n_{2})

\displaystyle=m_{0}\otimes_{H}n_{0}S(m_{1}n_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(m_{2})n_{2}+m_{0}\otimes_{H}n_{0}S(m_{1}n_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}\mathrm{d}(n_{2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\mathrm{d}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}^{N}(n)~.

∎

The right hand side of equation (
4.13
) can be understood as defining the
sum of the connections
\mathrm{d}^{M}
and
\mathrm{d}^{N}
; this equals the canonical
connection on
M\otimes_{H}N
. Since any right connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
on
M
is equal to the sum of
\mathrm{d}^{M}
and a
right
H
-linear map
\phi^{M}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

the sum of two arbitrary right
connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)

on the bicovariant bimodules
M,N
that we rewrite as

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}=\mathrm{d}^{M}+({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})=\mathrm{d}^{M}+\phi^{M}
,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}=\mathrm{d}^{N}+({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})=\mathrm{d}^{N}+\phi^{N}
,
is then given by

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}:=\mathrm{d}^{M\otimes_{H}N}+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N})+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))~.

(4.14)

Since
\phi^{M}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M}
and
\phi^{N}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}

are right
H
-linear maps, the tensor products
\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}

are well-defined and give right
H
-linear maps. It is immediate that
(
4.14
) defines a right
H
-connection
M\otimes_{H}N\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
and generalizes the sum (
4.13
)
of the canonical connection.
In order to formulate a sum of connections solely from the
initial data
({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})
and
without reference to the canonical connections
we employ the tensor product
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
of rational morphisms
defined in Proposition
2.14
via the lifting
\sigma
of
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
.

Theorem 4.19
.

Let
M,N
be modules of
{}_{H}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
, finitely generated as right

H
-modules and consider right connections

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)
.
Then

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}:=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}

(4.15)

defines a right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M\otimes_{H}N)
.
On elements
m\otimes_{H}n\in M\otimes_{H}N
this reads

({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})(m\otimes_{H}n)=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}n)~.

(4.16)

It follows that

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}^{M\otimes_{H}N}~,

(4.17)

i.e. definition (
4.15
) coincides with definition (
4.14
)
of the sum of connections.

Proof.

According to Proposition
2.11

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
are rational
morphisms, since
M,N
are finitely generated
H
-modules.
Then Theorem
2.14
implies that

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}

is well-defined as a linear map

M\otimes_{H}N\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
.
Employing (
2.19
)
the explicit expression (
4.16
) follows. Then, for all
m\otimes_{H}n\in M\otimes_{H}N

and
a\in H
,

\displaystyle({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})((m\otimes_{H}n)a)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}na)+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}na)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)a+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}n)a

\displaystyle\quad+m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}n\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})(m\otimes_{H}n))a+m\otimes_{H}n\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a

by (
4.2
) and the right
H
-linearity of
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
.
This proves that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
is a right connection on
M\otimes_{H}N
.
For the last statement we first prove that
