\displaystyle(\mathrm{d}^{M}\oplus\mathrm{d}^{N})(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=(\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(\mathrm{d}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N}))(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\mathrm{d}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)+\mathrm{d}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S(\mathrm{d}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=0+m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}^{N}(m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}n_{0}S(m_{3}n_{1})\otimes\mathrm{d}(m_{4}n_{2})

\displaystyle=(m_{0}\otimes_{H}n_{0})S(m_{1}n_{1})\otimes\mathrm{d}(m_{2}n_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}^{M\otimes_{H}N}(m\otimes_{H}n)

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
, using
\mathrm{d}^{M}(M^{\mathrm{co}H})=0

and that
\mathrm{d}^{N}
is left coinvariant
(cf. Proposition
4.2
).
Then (
4.17
) follows from the linearity of

\,\hat{\otimes}\,
and Theorem
2.14
.
This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

For two connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)

we call (
4.15
) or equivalently (
4.14
) the
sum of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}

and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
, written
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M\otimes_{H}N)
.
If
M=N
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
we abbreviate the sum of connections by

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot):={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M^{\otimes_{H}2})

and similarly for higher tensor products.
In analogy to (
4.15
) for
right
H
-linear maps
\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

and
\psi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(N,N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

we define the
sum of
H
-linear maps

\phi\oplus\psi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M\otimes_{H}N,M\otimes_{H}N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

by

\phi\oplus\psi:=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(\phi\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\psi~.

(4.18)

If
M=N
and
\phi=\psi
, we simply write
\phi(\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot):=\phi\oplus\phi\colon M^{\otimes_{H}2}\rightarrow M^{\otimes_{H}2}\otimes\Omega^{1}
.

Remark 4.20

(Bimodule Connections)
.

Usually for the sum of connections extra properties are required,
typically bimodule connections are considered
[
17
]
.
The sum of two
bimodule right connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)
is the
bimodule right connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}_{BM}(m\otimes_{H}n):=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+m\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}(n)~,

where
m\in M
and
n\in N
.
We
have seen that in the context of bicovariant bimodules arbitrary
right connections can be summed.
If
M,N
are bicovariant bimodules and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
is furthermore left

H
-colinear the connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}_{BM}

coincides with the sum of connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}

introduced in (
4.15
) since

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=(\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(m_{0}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)

\displaystyle~~+m_{0}S(m_{1})m_{2}\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}(n)+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+m\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}(n)

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}_{BM}(m\otimes_{H}n)

(4.19)

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
by the right Leibniz rule of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}
, the
additional left Leibniz rule (
4.3
) of the bimodule right connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
and
H
-colinearity:
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}=1\otimes{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
.

In particular, since the canonical connections are
H
-bicolinear, the sum of connections in (
4.13
) equals the sum of bimodule connections.

Remark 4.21

(Classical sum of connections)
.

Let
H
be a commutative Hopf algebra (e.g.
the coordinate algebra of a compact Lie group
G
) and
M,N
be symmetric
bicovariant bimodules, i.e.
am=ma
and
an=na
for all
a\in H
,
m\in M
and

n\in N
. In particular Woronowicz’s braiding
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,N}
coincides with the usual flip operator

\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}\colon M\otimes_{H}N\rightarrow N\otimes_{H}M
. Any right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

is a bimodule connection in this case, since

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(am)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(ma)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a=a{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)+\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},M}(\mathrm{d}a\otimes_{H}m)

for all
a\in H
and
m\in M
.

Furthermore, when considering the sum

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}(m\otimes_{H}n)
in equation (
4.19
) we have
that the equality

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}m_{0}S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}(m_{2}n)=m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}(m_{2}n)
holds because of commutativity, so that there is no need to require

H
-colinearity of
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}
. Therefore the sum of connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M\otimes_{H}N}

becomes the usual sum of connections of classical differential geometry:

({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})(m\otimes_{H}n)=\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m)\otimes_{H}n)+m\otimes_{H}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}(n)
.

4.5
Sum of Curvatures

In this section we give an explicit expression of the curvature of the sum of
connections in terms of the curvature of the initial connections.
We fix a bicovariant differential calculus
(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})
on

H
and consider bicovariant bimodules
M,M^{\prime},\ldots,N,N^{\prime},\ldots
, which are
finitely generated as right
H
-modules.
Recalling (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) the curvature
of a right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
on
M
reads

\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\bullet}\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}=\wedge_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}~.

(4.20)

Before we attack the sum of curvatures we study the compatibility
properties of the tensor product
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
with the composition
of rational morphisms.
Recall that for right
H
-linear maps
\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})
,

\phi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(M,M^{\prime})
,
\psi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime})
and

\psi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(N,N^{\prime})
the equality

(\phi\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\psi)\circ(\phi^{\prime}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\psi^{\prime})=(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}\phi^{\prime})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}^{\alpha}\psi\circ\psi^{\prime})

holds. More in general, recalling the lifting
\sigma
defined in (
2.16
) we have

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
\phi\in\mathrm{HOM}(M^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})
,
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{HOM}(M,M^{\prime})
,

\psi\in\mathrm{HOM}(N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime})
and
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{HOM}(N,N^{\prime})
.
Then

(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M^{\prime}}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi^{\prime})=(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi^{\prime})~~\mbox{
\it{and} }~~(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi^{\prime})=(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi\circ\psi^{\prime})~.

If
\phi^{\prime}
is right
H
-linear or right
H
-colinear we
further have

(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})\circ(\phi^{\prime}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})=(\phi\circ\phi^{\prime})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}~.

If
\phi^{\prime}
is right
H
-linear or, if
\phi^{\prime}
is right
H
-colinear
and
\psi
is left
H
-colinear, we have

(\mathrm{id}_{M^{\prime}}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)\circ(\phi^{\prime}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}})={}_{\alpha}\phi^{\prime}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi~.

Finally, the last two equalities hold also if

M^{\prime}=M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
,

\phi^{\prime}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
is a right connection
and, for the last one, if
\psi
is left
H
-colinear.

Proof.

The first equality easily follows from that for
\otimes_{\sigma}
in equation
(
2.20
) and recalling that
\phi\otimes_{\sigma}\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}}=(\phi\otimes_{\sigma}\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}})\circ\pi_{H}^{M,N^{\prime}}
. We prove at once this and all the other equalities for

\phi,\phi^{\prime},\psi,\psi^{\prime}
rational morphisms by
computing

\displaystyle((\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)\circ(\phi^{\prime}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi^{\prime}))(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)(\phi^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1}))\otimes_{H}\psi^{\prime}_{0}(m_{2}n))

\displaystyle=\phi(\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1}))_{0}S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1}))_{1}))

\displaystyle\quad\otimes_{H}\psi_{0}(\phi^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1}))_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{0}(m_{2}n))

\displaystyle=\phi\bigg(\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{0}\bigg(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1})\bigg)S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{1})\bigg)\otimes_{H}\psi_{0}(\phi^{\prime}_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{0}(m_{2}n))~,

and

\displaystyle((\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}^{\alpha}\psi\circ\psi^{\prime}))(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\big((\phi\circ(\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{0}S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{1})))\,\hat{\otimes}\,((\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{2})\circ\psi^{\prime})\big)(m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\phi((\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{0}S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{1}))([(\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{2})\circ\psi^{\prime}]_{-2}m_{0}S([(\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{2})\circ\psi^{\prime}]_{-1}m_{1})))\otimes_{H}[(\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{2})\circ\psi^{\prime}]_{0}(m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=\phi((\psi_{-4}\phi^{\prime}_{0}S(\psi_{-3}\phi^{\prime}_{1}))(\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{3}\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1})))\otimes_{H}[(\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{4})\circ\psi^{\prime}_{0}](m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=\phi(\psi_{-4}\phi^{\prime}_{0}(S(\psi_{-3}\phi^{\prime}_{1})\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{3}\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1})))\otimes_{H}[(\psi_{0}\phi^{\prime}_{4})\circ\psi^{\prime}_{0}](m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=\phi\bigg(\psi_{-2}\phi^{\prime}_{0}\bigg(\psi^{\prime}_{-2}m_{0}S(\psi^{\prime}_{-1}m_{1})S(\psi_{-1}\phi^{\prime}_{1})\bigg)\bigg)\otimes_{H}\psi_{0}(\phi^{\prime}_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{0}(m_{2}n))

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
. We see that these expressions coincide
if
\phi^{\prime}
is right
H
-linear or if
\phi^{\prime}
is right
H
-colinear and

\psi
is left
H
-colinear. As special cases we obtain the equalities
displayed in the lemma.

Assume that
M^{\prime}=M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
and

\phi^{\prime}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
is a right connection.
Note that
\mathrm{d}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi^{\prime}=0
for all linear maps

\psi^{\prime}
because the canonical connection
\mathrm{d}^{M}
vanishes on
M^{\mathrm{co}H}
.
Then

\displaystyle(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi)\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}-\mathrm{d}^{M}))\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi

\displaystyle=(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi-(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi

\displaystyle=(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi-(\phi\circ(\psi_{-2}\cdot\mathrm{d}^{M}\cdot S(\psi_{-1})))\,\hat{\otimes}\,\psi_{0}

\displaystyle=(\phi\circ{}_{\alpha}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}^{\alpha}\psi~,

where in the last two equations we have used that
\mathrm{d}^{M}
is right
H
-colinear and
assumed that
\psi
is left
H
-colinear. The final part of the lemma follows again by
specializing this equality.
∎

Theorem 4.23
.

Consider two right connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)
.
The curvature of their sum
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M\otimes_{H}N)
defined in Theorem
4.19

is given by

\begin{split}\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}&=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{M})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}\\
&\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ\big(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\big)\\
&\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}\big)\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N})~,\end{split}

(4.21)

where all addends are right
H
-linear maps

M\otimes_{H}N\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2}
.

Proof.

We split the proof into seven parts.

i.)

Firstly, by (
4.20
) and observing that

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}
is a right
H
-linear map we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})]\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

(4.22)

\displaystyle\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ[[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})]\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})

(4.23)

\displaystyle\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ[(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

(4.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ[(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})

(4.25)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

(4.26)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})~.

(4.27)

In the rest of the proof we identify these six terms with the right hand side of
(
4.21
).

ii.)

Utilizing the associativity of
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
(Proposition
2.14
),
Lemma
4.22
and the fact that
\,\hat{\otimes}\,
is a morphism of bicovariant
bimodules (again Proposition
2.14
) it follows that

\displaystyle(\ref{SOC4})+(\ref{SOC6})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})]\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})+(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[(\mathrm{id}_{M}\circ{}_{\alpha}\mathrm{id}_{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}^{\alpha}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})]+[\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,(\mathrm{id}_{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})]\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[[\mathrm{id}_{M}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-2}\cdot\mathrm{id}_{M}\cdot S({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{-1}))]\,\hat{\otimes}\,[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}_{0}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}]]+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\circ{}_{\alpha}\mathrm{id}_{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}^{\alpha}(\mathrm{id}_{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}]]+\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,[(\mathrm{id}_{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,[\wedge_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}+(\mathrm{id}_{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}~.

iii.)

For
\phi\in\mathrm{HOM}(M,M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})
a rational morphism,
the left
H
-linearity of
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
implies the identity

(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{34}\circ(\phi\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

of right
H
-linear maps

M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}N\rightarrow M\otimes\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}N
.
Recalling that

\wedge=\mathrm{id}-\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
and the
braid relation
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{12}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{22}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{12}=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{12}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}
it is also easy to see that

\wedge_{23}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1},M}=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{2},M}\circ\wedge_{12}

as an equation of bicovariant bimodule morphisms

\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}M\rightarrow M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{2}
. These
relations, with
\phi={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}
, imply

\displaystyle(\ref{SOC1})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ[[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})]\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}]\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{34}\circ[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N}]\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23,4}\circ[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N}]\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ\wedge_{23}\circ[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ\wedge_{23}\circ[[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}]\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[[\wedge_{23}\circ[({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}]]\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]~.

iv.)

Let
m\in M
and
n\in N
. We introduce the short notation

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes m_{2}=:\mathcal{M}\otimes_{H}\Theta\otimes m_{2}
, where we omitted writing explicitly the dependence on
m_{0}S(m_{1})
in
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{H}\Theta
.
From

\displaystyle[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[((\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}[(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1})))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\mathcal{M}_{0}S(\mathcal{M}_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{M}_{2}\Theta)\otimes_{H}m_{2}n)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}S(\mathcal{M}_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathcal{M}_{2}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\mathcal{M}_{3}\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{M}_{4}\Theta_{0})(m_{2}n)_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\mathcal{M}_{1}\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{M}_{2}\Theta_{0})(m_{2}n)_{2}

and

\displaystyle[\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ

\displaystyle({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N})](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=[\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}})](m_{0}S(m_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}^{N}(m_{2}n))

\displaystyle=[\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}]({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}(m_{2}n)_{0}S((m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}((m_{2}n)_{2}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S((m_{2}n)_{1})S(\Theta_{-1})\otimes_{H}\Theta_{0}\wedge\mathrm{d}((m_{2}n)_{2})

it follows that

\displaystyle(\ref{SOC5})

\displaystyle=[(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})(\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}(m_{0}S(m_{1}))\otimes_{H}m_{2}n))

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})[\mathcal{M}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\Theta_{0}(m_{2}n)_{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})S(\mathcal{M}_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{M}_{2}\Theta_{0}(m_{2}n)_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\mathcal{M}_{1}\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}(\mathcal{M}_{2}\Theta_{0})(m_{2}n)_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{M}\otimes_{H}\Theta_{-2}(m_{2}n)_{0}S(\Theta_{-1}(m_{2}n)_{1})\otimes_{H}\Theta_{0}\wedge\mathrm{d}((m_{2}n)_{2})

\displaystyle=[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[((\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle\quad-[\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N})](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle=[\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[((\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]](m\otimes_{H}n)

\displaystyle\quad-[\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N})](m\otimes_{H}n)~.

v.)

Adding the results of iii.) and iv.) gives

\displaystyle\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ[[\wedge_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}]\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}]

\displaystyle=(\ref{SOC1})+(\ref{SOC5})+\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N}).

vi.)

Since
\mathrm{d}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N}=0
it follows that

\displaystyle\wedge_{34}\circ

\displaystyle\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))

\displaystyle\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))

\displaystyle\quad+\wedge_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))+\wedge_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}))\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=(\ref{SOC2})+(\ref{SOC3})-\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\,\hat{\otimes}\,\mathrm{d}^{N})~.

vii.)

Combining ii.), v.) and vi.), the claim (
4.21
) follows.

∎

Remark 4.24

(Classical Sum of Curvatures)
.

For a commutative Hopf algebra
H
and two right connections
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)
,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(N)
on symmetric bicovariant bimodules

M,N
we verify that (
4.21
) gives back the formulas
known from differential geometry. Let
m\in M
and
n\in N
.
We first recall that right
H
-linearity is equivalent to left
H
-linearity in
this setting. Then
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}=\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}
becomes
the tensor product of right
H
-linear maps
\phi
and
\psi
between symmetric
bicovariant bimodules:

\phi\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\psi=\phi\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}}\psi=\psi\otimes_{H}\phi

that we denoted as usual

\psi\otimes_{H}\phi
. Furthermore

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}))\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{H}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{23}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}}\otimes_{H}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))~.

Now consider the last term on the
right hand side of (
4.21
). Since

\wedge\circ\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}=-\wedge
we obtain

\displaystyle\wedge_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{H}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{23}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=\wedge_{34}\circ\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{34}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{M\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}}\otimes_{H}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{N})

\displaystyle=-\wedge_{34}\circ(({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}-\mathrm{d}^{M})\otimes_{H}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}-\mathrm{d}^{N}))

for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
,
i.e. the last two terms on the right hand side of (
4.21
) cancel and

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}=\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{23}\circ(\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{M}}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{N})+\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{R}^{{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{N}}

remains. So the curvature of the sum of connections is the sum of the individual curvatures.

5
Riemannian Geometry on Quantum Groups

In this section we fix a Woronowicz differential calculus

(\Omega^{\bullet},\wedge,\mathrm{d})

(cf. Proposition
3.6
). We further fix
a module
M
of
{}_{H}^{H}\mathcal{M}_{H}^{H}
which is finitely
generated as a right
H
-module and
assume that
\overline{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}_{M,M}

is diagonalisable with
\lambda=1
among its eigenvalues. Following Lemma
3.3
there is a direct sum decomposition

M^{2}=M\otimes_{H}M=(M\vee M)\oplus(M\wedge M)

of bicovariant bimodules, where
M\vee M:=M^{\vee 2}:=V_{1}
is the eigenspace
corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda=1
and
M\wedge M:=M^{\wedge 2}:=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{1\}}V_{\lambda}
the direct sum
of all other eigenspaces, the braided exterior algebra in degree two. The projectors

P_{\vee}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}

and
P_{\wedge}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}

are morphism of bicovariant bimodules.
Dually, there is the decomposition

(M^{*})^{2}=(M^{*})^{\vee 2}\oplus(M^{*})^{\wedge 2}

of bicovariant bimodules,
where
(M^{*})^{\vee 2}=V^{*}_{1}
,
(M^{*})^{\wedge 2}=\bigoplus\nolimits_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{1\}}V^{*}_{\lambda}
and the projectors

P^{*}_{\vee}=(P_{\vee})^{*}
,
P^{*}_{\wedge}=(P_{\wedge})^{*}
, or more generally

P^{*}_{\lambda}=(P_{\lambda})^{*}
, are the transposed of the projectors
P_{\lambda}
(see again Lemma
3.3
). Let us denote the corresponding
bicovariant bimodule projections by

\pi_{\vee}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M\vee M
,

\pi_{\wedge}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M\wedge M
,

\pi^{*}_{\vee}\colon(M^{*})^{2}\rightarrow M^{*}\vee M^{*}

and
\pi^{*}_{\wedge}\colon(M^{*})^{2}\rightarrow M^{*}\wedge M^{*}
.

Consider now a right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M)

and its extension
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
via the sum of connections
(
4.15
).
The following lemma shows that this sum of connections induces a right connection
on
M\vee M
, a right connection on
M\wedge M
and
two right
H
-linear maps. These connections and
H
-linear maps
determine the sum of connections completely.

Lemma 5.1
.

A right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}

induces two right connections

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}:=(\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}|_{M^{\vee 2}}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M^{\vee 2})

(5.1)

and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}:=(\pi_{\wedge}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}|_{M^{\wedge 2}}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(M^{\wedge 2})

(5.2)

on
M^{\vee 2}
and
M^{\wedge 2}
, respectively.
Furthermore, it induces two right
H
-linear maps,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}:=(\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}|_{M^{\wedge 2}}\colon M^{\wedge 2}\rightarrow M^{\vee 2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}

(5.3)

and

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}:=(\pi_{\wedge}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}|_{M^{\vee 2}}\colon M^{\vee 2}\rightarrow M^{\wedge 2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}~.

(5.4)

On the other hand, two right connections (
5.1
) and (
5.2
), together with
two right
H
-linear maps (
5.3
) and (
5.4
),
completely determine a right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}

by

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}\circ\pi_{\vee}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}\circ\pi_{\wedge}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}\circ\pi_{\wedge}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}\circ\pi_{\vee}~,

(5.5)

or, in matrix notation,
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}=\begin{pmatrix}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}&{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}\\
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}&{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}\end{pmatrix}
.

An analogous statement holds for left connections.

Proof.

Let
m\in M^{\vee 2}
and
a\in H
. Since
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
satisfies the Leibniz rule
and
\pi_{\vee}
is right
H
-linear,

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(ma)=(\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a
, i.e.
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}
is a right connection on

M^{\vee 2}
. In complete analogy, one proves that

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}
is a right connection on
M^{\wedge 2}
. Again for

m\in M^{\vee 2}
and
a\in H
we obtain

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}(ma)=(\pi_{\wedge}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(m)a+m\otimes_{H}\mathrm{d}a)={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}(m)a
, i.e.
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}
is right
H
-linear, and similarly
for
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}
.

On the other hand, the data (
5.1
)-(
5.4
) determines a right connection
on
M^{2}
via (
5.5
), since

\displaystyle{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}

\displaystyle=((\pi_{\vee}+\pi_{\wedge})\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\circ(\pi_{\vee}+\pi_{\wedge})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\circ\pi_{\vee}+(\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\circ\pi_{\wedge}

\displaystyle\quad+(\pi_{\wedge}\circ\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}){}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\circ\pi_{\vee}+(\pi_{\wedge}\circ\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}){}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\circ\pi_{\wedge}

\displaystyle={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}\circ\pi_{\vee}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}\circ\pi_{\wedge}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}\circ\pi_{\vee}+{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}\circ\pi_{\wedge}~.

∎

Classically,
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}=0={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}
and thus
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon M^{2}\rightarrow M^{2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
simply restricts to connections
on
M^{\vee 2}
and
M^{\wedge 2}
.
In the rest of this section we focus on
M=\Omega^{1}
. As before we
denote the dual bicovariant bimodule by
\mathfrak{X}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1},H)
.

Let
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})
be a right connection, consider the
corresponding sum

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})

defined in (
4.15
) and denote the
right connections and right
H
-linear maps induced from
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}

via Lemma
5.1
by

\begin{split}{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}((\Omega^{1})^{\vee 2})~,&~~~~~{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}((\Omega^{1})^{\wedge 2},(\Omega^{1})^{\vee 2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})~,\\
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}((\Omega^{1})^{\wedge 2})~,&~~~~~{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}((\Omega^{1})^{\vee 2},(\Omega^{1})^{\wedge 2}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})~.\end{split}

(5.6)

Following Proposition
4.16

iv.)
there is an induced left connection

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\oplus{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Con}(\mathfrak{X}^{2})
dual to
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\oplus{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
.
Using the left linear analogue of Lemma
5.1
we obtain left connections
and left
H
-linear maps

\begin{split}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2})~,&~~~~~{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{12}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{X}^{\wedge 2},\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2})~,\\
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\mathfrak{X}^{\wedge 2})~,&~~~~~{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{21}\in{}_{H}\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2},\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}^{\wedge 2})~.\end{split}

(5.7)

The following lemma proves the duality of (
5.6
) and (
5.7
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})
be a right connection
and consider the induced connections and
H
-linear maps in
(
5.6
) and (
5.7
).
Then
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}
and
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}
are the dual connections of

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}
, respectively, and

\begin{split}\langle X_{\vee},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}(\omega_{\wedge})\rangle&=-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{21}(X_{\vee}),\omega_{\wedge}\rangle~,\\
\langle X_{\wedge},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}(\omega_{\vee})\rangle&=-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{12}(X_{\wedge}),\omega_{\vee}\rangle\end{split}

for all
X_{\vee}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
,
X_{\wedge}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\wedge 2}
,

\omega_{\vee}\in\Omega^{\vee 2}
and
\omega_{\wedge}\in\Omega^{\wedge 2}
.

Proof.

For
X_{\vee}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
,

\omega_{\vee}\in\Omega^{\vee 2}

we have that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}
is the dual connection to
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}
,
since

\displaystyle\langle X_{\vee},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(\omega_{\vee})\rangle=\langle X_{\vee},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(\omega_{\vee})\rangle=\mathrm{d}\langle X_{\vee},\omega_{\vee}\rangle-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(X_{\vee}),\omega_{\vee}\rangle=\mathrm{d}\langle X_{\vee},\omega_{\vee}\rangle-\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(X_{\vee}),\omega_{\vee}\rangle

and similarly one proves that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\wedge}
are dual.
We further have

\displaystyle\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{21}(X_{\vee}),\omega_{\wedge}\rangle=\langle{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(X_{\vee}),\omega_{\wedge}\rangle=\mathrm{d}\underbrace{\langle X_{\vee},\omega_{\wedge}\rangle}_{=0}-\langle X_{\vee},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}(\omega_{\wedge})\rangle=-\langle X_{\vee},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{12}(\omega_{\wedge})\rangle~,

where
\omega_{\wedge}\in\Omega^{\wedge 2}

and similarly one proves that
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{12}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{21}
are dual.
∎

Extending the onion-like pairing (
3.6
) and
evaluating it on

\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{g}^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{a}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{2}

(finite sum on the index
\mathrm{a}
understood) we obtain the right
H
-linear maps

\begin{split}&\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle\colon\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\to H~,~~\\[6.00006pt]
&\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle\colon\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\to\Omega^{1}~,~~\end{split}\begin{split}&\omega\otimes_{H}\eta\mapsto\langle\mathbf{g},\omega\otimes_{H}\eta\rangle=\langle\mathbf{g}^{\mathrm{a}}\langle\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{a}},\omega\rangle,\eta\rangle~,~~\\[6.00006pt]
&\omega\otimes_{H}\eta\otimes_{H}\rho\mapsto\langle\mathbf{g},\omega\otimes_{H}\eta\otimes\rho\rangle=\langle\mathbf{g}^{\mathrm{a}}\langle\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{a}},\omega\rangle,\eta\rangle\rho~.\end{split}

(5.8)

The restrictions to the bicovariant sub-bimodules

\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
and
(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}

with slight abuse of notation are denoted by

\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle\colon\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}\to H
and
\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle\colon(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\to\Omega^{1}
.

Definition 5.3

(Metric tensor and Levi-Civita connections)
.

a

i.)

An element
\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{g}^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{a}}\in\mathfrak{X}^{2}
(finite sum on the index
\mathrm{a}
understood) is said to be a
(pseudo-Riemannian) metric if
\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
(i.e.
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{X}}(\mathbf{g})=\mathbf{g}
) and

\mathbf{g}^{\sharp}:=\mathbf{g}^{\mathrm{a}}\langle\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{a}},\cdot\rangle\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}
is an isomorphism of right

H
-modules.

ii.)

A right connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})
is
said to be compatible with a pseudo-Riemannian metric

\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
, if
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(\mathbf{g})=0
, where

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
is the induced
connection on
\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
, i.e., if

\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}_{\vee}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle=\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle

as an equation of linear maps
\Omega\vee\Omega\to\Omega
.

If in addition
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})
is torsion-free we call it a
Levi-Civita connection with respect to
\mathbf{g}
.

Remark 5.4
.

We compare Definition
5.3
to other notions of metric and
Levi-Civita connection in the literature.

i.)

If
H
is commutative and
\Omega^{1}
a symmetric bicovariant
bimodule (in particular

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}}=\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}}
)
Definition
5.3
reproduces the classical notions of metric and
Levi-Civita connection. The metric axioms become

\sigma^{\mathrm{flip}}_{\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{X}}(\mathbf{g})=\mathbf{g}
and
that contraction with
\mathbf{g}
determines an isomorphism of the cotangent
and tangent bundle
\mathbf{g}^{\#}\colon\Omega^{1}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathfrak{X}
. For the Levi-Civita
condition we first note that in the commutative case
the sum of connections is the canonical
tensor product extension of a connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}

according to Remark
4.20
.
Since
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{12}={\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{21}=0
, the condition

{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(\mathbf{g})=0
,
\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
, given in Definition
5.3
,
is equivalent to the classical metric compatibility
{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}(\mathbf{g})=0
.
In particular, the notions of pseudo-Riemannian metric and Levi-Civita
connection in differential geometry is covered.
More generally, Definition
5.3
encompasses the Levi-Civita condition of braided symmetric
Riemannian geometry studied in
[
3
,
32
]
, as we will discuss in the next subsection.

ii.)

The definition of metrics in Definition
5.3
generalizes
the notion of metrics on Hopf algebras in
[
7
, Def. 1.15]
.
There, the isomorphism
\mathbf{g}^{\#}
is assumed to be an
H
-bimodule map,
which implies that
\mathbf{g}
is a central element.

Given a pseudo-Riemannian metric
\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}

we define a linear map

\begin{split}\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})&\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1})~,\\
\phi&\mapsto\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi):=\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle\circ\phi_{\vee}~,\end{split}

(5.9)

where
\phi_{\vee}
is defined as the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\oplus\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\vee}\otimes_{H}\mathrm{id}}

and
\phi\oplus\phi
denotes
the sum of right
H
-linear maps

\phi\oplus\phi=\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}\circ(\phi\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}})+\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\phi\colon\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}\rightarrow(\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}~,

cf. (
4.18
).

Lemma 5.5
.

The map in (
5.9
) is well-defined and right
H
-linear. Moreover,
the identity

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)=2\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{1}}\otimes_{\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}}\phi)|_{\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}}

(5.10)

holds as an equality of linear maps
\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}\to\Omega^{1}
.

Proof.

As a composition of right
H
-linear maps
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)
is right
H
-linear:
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}a)=\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})a

for all
\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}\in\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
(finite sum over the index
\mathrm{a}
understood) and elements
a\in H
,
hence
\Phi
is well-defined. Right
H
-linearity of
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
, i.e.

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi\cdot a)(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})=\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)(a\cdot(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}))

immediately follows from the identity (
5.10
) that we now prove.
For all
\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}\in\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
(finite sum over the index
\mathrm{a}
understood), recalling that
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{X}}(\mathbf{g})=\mathbf{g}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}(\phi)(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\phi(\omega^{\mathrm{a}})\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})+{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{X}}(\mathbf{g}),\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{23}(\phi(\omega^{\mathrm{a}})\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle+\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{12,3}(\phi(\omega^{\mathrm{a}})\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}})+{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\alpha}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}{}^{\alpha}(\phi(\omega^{\mathrm{a}}))+{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\alpha\beta}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)({}^{\beta}\omega^{\mathrm{a}})+{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle

\displaystyle=2\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\alpha}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}({}^{\alpha}\phi)(\omega_{\mathrm{a}})\rangle~,

where we used Lemma
3.2

and
{}_{\beta}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}{}^{\beta}\omega^{\mathrm{a}}=\omega^{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{H}\omega_{\mathrm{a}}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

i.)

The definition in equation (
5.9
) was inspired by

[
11
, Def. 7.2]
. There, however, only bicoinvariant
metrics were considered, while
(
5.9
) is
well-defined for arbitrary metrics.

ii.)

Due to the
arbitrariness of the metric
\mathbf{g}
we can consider
conformally equivalent metrics
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}=f\mathbf{g}
with
f\in H
invertible. We then observe that

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is left
H
-linear in
\mathbf{g}
:

\Phi_{f\mathbf{g}}=\ell_{f}\circ\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
, where

\ell_{f}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1})
,

\phi\mapsto(f\cdot\phi)
.

iii.)

Using Lemma
3.3
the map
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
in equation
(
5.9
) can be identified with the right
H
-linear map

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}:(\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})\otimes_{H}\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}(\mathfrak{X}\vee\mathfrak{X})~.

Domain and codomain of this right
H
-linear map are free finitely
generated modules, as follows recalling the discussion after
(
3.8
) and Lemma
3.3
i.). Thus, upon choosing a basis, the invertibility
of
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is the invertibility of a finite-dimensional matrix
with values in
H
.

It turns out that existence and uniqueness of the Levi-Civita
connection for a given arbitrary metric
\mathbf{g}
is proven provided

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is invertible. The connection is
explicitly described in terms of the structure constant
connection
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}\in\mathrm{Con}_{H}(\Omega^{1})
discussed in
Proposition
4.8

and the inverse of the
H
-linear map
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
.

Theorem 5.7
.

Consider a pseudo-Riemannian metric

\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
. If
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is an
isomorphism of vector spaces then

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}:={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}+\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}\bigg(\bigg(\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot)\rangle\bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}}\bigg)

(5.11)

is the unique Levi-Civita connection for
\mathbf{g}
.
Surjectivity of
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
provides existence while injectivity of

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
ensures uniqueness of the Levi-Civita connection.

Proof.

We prove that (
5.11
) is a Levi-Civita connection for
\mathbf{g}
.
First of all, the map

\psi:=\bigg(\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\cdot\otimes_{H}\cdot)\rangle\bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}}\colon\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}

is right
H
-linear since

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\langle\mathbf{g},\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}a\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}a)\rangle

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}\langle\mathbf{g},\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}\rangle)a+\langle\mathbf{g},\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}\rangle\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle\quad-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i})\rangle a-\langle\mathbf{g},\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}\rangle\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{d}\langle\mathbf{g},\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}\rangle)a-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i})\rangle a

for all
a\in H
and
\omega^{i}\otimes_{H}\omega_{i}\in\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
.
By assumption
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is invertible and

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}(\psi)\in\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1})\subseteq\mathrm{Hom}_{H}(\Omega^{1},\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1})~.

It follows that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}+\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}(\psi)
is a right connection on

\Omega^{1}
.

Then

\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}=\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}+\wedge\circ\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}(\psi)=\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}=-\mathrm{d}

since
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}(\psi))\subseteq\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}
is
torsion-free. This implies that
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}
is a torsion-free right connection.

Furthermore,
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}
is compatible with
\mathbf{g}
, since

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{\mathrm{LC}}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle+\langle\mathbf{g},\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}(\psi)_{\vee}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle+\psi(\cdot\vee\cdot)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle+\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle+\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{c}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle

is an equation of linear maps
\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}
.
This proves the existence of a Levi-Civita connection for
\mathbf{g}
.

Assume that there is another Levi-Civita connection

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
for
\mathbf{g}
. Then

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
is a
right
H
-linear map. Since
\wedge\circ({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})=\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-\wedge\circ{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}=-\mathrm{d}+\mathrm{d}=0
we can view
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
as a right
H
-linear map

\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
. Consequently, we can apply

\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
which results in the zero map:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}({}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!})

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}^{\mathrm{LC}}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle-\langle\mathbf{g},{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}_{\vee}(\cdot\vee\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle-\mathrm{d}\circ\langle\mathbf{g},\cdot\vee\cdot\rangle

\displaystyle=0\colon\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}~,

where we used that both
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}
and
{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}
are
compatible with
\mathbf{g}
.
Since
\Phi_{\mathbf{g}}
is injective this implies that

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}-{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}=0\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\vee\Omega^{1}
, i.e.

{}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}{}^{\mathrm{LC}}={}_{\,}\reflectbox{\mbox{${\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1.45}[-1]{\mbox{$\mathbb{\Delta}$}}}}_{\!}$}}_{\!}\colon\Omega^{1}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}\otimes_{H}\Omega^{1}
.
This proves uniqueness of the Levi-Civita connection.
∎

We next apply this theorem to a certain class of metrics that
we call
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
-central metrics
(named

\sigma
-metrics on self-dual bicovariant calculi in
[
18
]
);
we also clarify their relation to central metrics.

Definition 5.8
.

We say that a metric
\mathbf{g}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}
is

i.)

central if
a\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{g}a
for all
a\in H
,

ii.)

\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
-central if
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2},\Omega^{1}}(\mathbf{g}\otimes_{H}\omega)=\omega\otimes_{H}\mathbf{g}
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Omega^{1},\mathfrak{X}^{\vee 2}}(\omega\otimes_{H}\mathbf{g})=\mathbf{g}\otimes_{H}\omega
for all
\omega\in\Omega^{1}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Every
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
-central metric is central and every central
H
-bicoinvariant
metric is
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
-central.

Proof.

For the first claim consider a basis
\{\omega^{i}\}_{i\in I}
of

{}^{\mathrm{co}H}\Omega^{1}
and an arbitrary element
a\in H
. By the
H
-linearity of
the braiding and the assumption that
\mathbf{g}
is
\sigma^{\mathcal{W}}
-central
we obtain
